*O divine vineyard, who didst bud forth the beautiful cluster and dost give divine drink unto souls, O Virgin: from bitter drink, and the drunkenness of passions and pleasures, and eternal fire, deliver my soul.*[^3375]

*Troparion 1:* Pluck me out of the mire of sins, O most pure Bride of God, who have fallen into the filth of passions; and having washed me from the defilements of passions with the streams of thy prayers, clothe me with the light-bearing robe of salvation.[^3376]

*Troparion 2:* O pure Virgin, who hast granted peace unto the world and salvation unto all, having borne the divine Peace: pacify the present warfare of passions of my soul and body, by the fear and love of Christ the Savior.[^3377]

*Troparion 3:* Heal by thy tenderheartedness my soul that is sick with sins, O Most Pure One, and direct me ever to do the commandments of thy Son in humility, that I may receive His goodness.[^3378]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*The elder cried aloud unto Thee, having seen with his eyes the salvation which came from God unto the peoples: O Christ, Thou art my God.*[^3379]

*Troparion 1:* Thou wast slaughtered willingly as a lamb, O Christ, raising again unto life him who through eating of the tree had died willingly.[^3380]

*Troparion 2:* Thou wast lifted up upon the Cross, and demonic delusion fell down; and the multitude of the faithful was exalted, hymning Thee, O Giver of life.[^3381]

*Martyricon 1:* Adorned with the purple robe dyed from their blood, the martyrs now stand before the King of all, decorated with radiant crowns.[^3382]

*Martyricon 2:* The relics of the holy martyrs pour forth healing unto all who approach...[^3383]

*On Friday at Matins, Tone 3.*

...and drown the multitude of passions.[^3384]

*Theotokion:* The human mind cannot comprehend, O Maiden, the mystery of thy childbirth beyond nature; for beyond understanding thou didst bear God.[^3385]

*Another Heirmos:*
*The uttermost abyss of sins hath surrounded me, and my spirit fails; but stretching forth Thy lofty arm, O Master, as Peter, O Helmsman, save me.*[^3386]

*Troparion 1:* Grant deliverance from transgressions, as thou art good, unto thy servant, O Most Pure One, who with faith asks thy protection, and snatch me away from the coming judgment.[^3387]

*Troparion 2:* O Lady and Mother of the Redeemer! Stand by me in the hour of my departure, when I am examined by the spirits of the air concerning the things which I have done with foolish mind.[^3388]

*Troparion 3:* All wretched have I been shown forth, ever held fast by defilements, and now beholding death behind my feet, I cry aloud unto thee, O Theotokos: Help me.[^3389]

*Troparion 4:* The waves of passionate thoughts ever agitate me, O Most Pure One, and the tempest of evil spirits drowns me; but establish me upon the rock of dispassion.[^3390]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*Unto Thee, Who in the fire didst bedew the children who theologized, and Who didst take up Thy dwelling in the incorrupt Virgin, we sing, God the Word, chanting piously: Blessed is the God of our fathers.*[^3391]

*Troparion 1:* Thou wast lifted up upon cedar, and pine, and cypress, O Master, being One of the Trinity, and Thou didst lift up those who had fallen into the depth of many pleasures: Blessed is the God of our fathers.[^3392]

*Troparion 2:* By Thy precious Blood, O Lord, Thou didst cleanse creation from the blood offered unto loathsome demons, and Thou didst destroy defiled sacrifices, O Word of God, having been sacrificed as a lamb. Glory to Thy dominion![^3393]

*Martyricon 1:* Standing before the torturers as immovable pillars, shaking delusion, and confirming the hearts of the faithful, the sufferers sang: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers.[^3394]

*Martyricon 2:* Ye were not consumed by fire, having acquired a resolve warmer than fire, O passion-bearers of Christ, crown-bearers, crying aloud: Blessed...[^3395]

*On Friday at Matins, Tone 3.*

...art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers.[^3396]

*Theotokion:*
An inconceivable conception and an ineffable childbirth thou didst have, O thou only Pure One, having incarnated the Master Who was crucified in the flesh; unto Whom chanting we cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers.[^3397]

*Another Heirmos:*
*As of old Thou didst bedew the three pious children in the Chaldean flame, with the radiant fire of the Godhead illuminate us also who cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.*[^3398]

*Troparion 1:* Unto thee, the Mother of God, pure and undefiled, I pray, defiled in body and soul, and wretched with unclean deeds; and I hope, O Lady, in thy mercy. Have compassion upon me, O Most Pure One.[^3399]

*Troparion 2:* The multitude of my transgressions and the trial of evil deeds grievously confounds me with perplexity, and draws me into the depth of despair. O most pure Lady, save me who am perishing and cruelly drowned.[^3400]

*Troparion 3:* Have mercy, O Good One, upon my soul which is brought low by evil deeds, and direct me unto the way of repentance, and guide me to do the desires of thy Son, and deliver me from torment.[^3401]

*Troparion 4:* By the multitude of the compassions of thy goodness, overlook the multitude of our sins, O Good One, and be well-disposed unto them that unceasingly cry aloud: Blessed, O Most Pure One, is the Fruit of thy womb.[^3402]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*United with unbearable fire, yet unharmed by the flame, the youths, champions of piety, sang the divine hymn: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.*[^3403]

*Troparion 1:* A disobedient and senseless people condemns Thee to die, Who with obedient disposition didst will to be crucified, that by Thy will Thou mightest make alive those who had been put to death, O Word, who sing of Thee and exalt Thee above all unto the ages.[^3404]

*Troparion 2:* Having spread out Thy palms upon the Cross, Thou healest the hands of the first-created intemperately stretched forth unto the edible tree, O Master! and seeing Thee, in fear the sun hid its rays, and all creation was shaken.[^3405]

*Martyricon:* By the rays of their sacred contests, the passion-bearers dried up the streams of impiety and the outpourings of godlessness...[^3406]

*On Friday at Matins, Tone 3.*

...and gushed forth fountains of healings, which wash away the defilement of passions, and richly water the hearts of the faithful.[^3407]

*Martyricon:* Conformed unto the precious Passion, and fulfillers of the divine commandments, O passion-bearers, fellow citizens of the bodiless hosts: pray unto God for us who honor you unto all ages, that we may now be inscribed in the city on high.[^3408]

*Theotokion:* As the golden jar and lampstand, the table and rod, the divine mountain and cloud, the palace of the King, and the fiery throne, we, all the faithful, name thee, O Theotokos, who wast preserved a virgin after childbirth.[^3409]

*Another Heirmos:*
*Him Who descended by divine power into the flame unto the Hebrew children, and appeared as Lord, ye priests bless, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.*[^3410]

*Troparion 1:* The bitterness of pleasures, having passed through all the bodily senses, unfittingly defiles my soul, and drags it unto death. O Lady of the world, be thou my salvation.[^3411]

*Troparion 2:* Upon thee have I placed my heart and soul and body, O Lady; for I have no other hope save thee, through whom I may obtain mercy. Therefore grant me thy rich grace.[^3412]

*Troparion 3:* The serpent, having crept near, with the poison of fleshly pleasure hath cruelly slain my wretched soul; but make it alive, O Pure One, by contrary remedies through thy prayers.[^3413]

*Troparion 4:* A grievous night of transgressions covers me, O holy Lady! for I have no lamp enlightening my soul with the oil of well-doing; therefore I was cast out of the bridechamber on high.[^3414]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*In the shadow and writings of the Law, we the faithful see a figure: every male that opens the womb is holy unto God. Therefore the first-born Word, the unoriginate Father's Son, being first-born of a Mother that knew not man, we magnify.*[^3415]

*Troparion 1:* Having been nailed upon the Cross, O Jesus, Who hast founded all the earth upon nothing: having had compassion as Thou art good, O Christ, Thou hast drawn me out who was stuck fast in the mire of sin by an evil disposition, and by Thy dishonorable death Thou hast honored me, O Plenteous in mercy.[^3416]

*Troparion 2:* Thou wast seen, God invisible by nature, being lifted up in the flesh, that from invisible foes Thou mightest deliver the visible...[^3417]

*On Friday at Matins, Tone 3.*

...world, and make those who are below heavenly, O Christ, who glorify the dominion of Thy great power.[^3418]

*Martyricon 1:* A sacred army, chosen like unto the holy angels, a paradise having in its midst the tree of life, Christ, a precious assembly of the divinely fair Church were ye shown forth, O all-glorious passion-bearers of the Savior.[^3419]

*Martyricon 2:* Remember us on earth who remember you, O holy ones, standing joyfully before the Master's throne; and with the rays from thence richly enlighten us, O sacred ones, that we may receive remission of debts.[^3420]

*Theotokion:* Seeing Thy crucifixion, O my Son, the luminaries set; and how did the faithless assembly of the Hebrews not set, having delivered Thee unto death, the Prince of life? the Theotokos cried aloud; whom unceasingly we magnify.[^3421]

*Another Heirmos:*
*Thee, the unburned bush, and holy Virgin, Mother of the Light, and Theotokos, the hope of us all, we magnify.*[^3422]

*Troparion 1:* Having cleansed the defilement of passionate thoughts of my mind, O Pure One, clothe me with the radiant robe of dispassion.[^3423]

*Troparion 2:* Open unto me the divine entrances of repentance, O Virgin, closing and barring the entrances of my passions and pleasures by thy power.[^3424]

*Troparion 3:* Give ear unto the voice of my groaning, and the voice of my weeping, O all-immaculate Virgin, and grant cleansing and salvation unto my wretched soul.[^3425]

*Troparion 4:* I, wretched one, have despaired entirely, and am at a loss, reflecting upon my evil deeds; only have compassion, O Lady, and save me.[^3426]

*Then: It is truly meet: litany, and Exapostilarion.*[^3427]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Cross, Tone 3.*
The tree of disobedience budded forth death unto the world, but the tree of the Cross life and incorruption. Therefore we pray Thee, the Lord Who wast crucified: let the light of Thy countenance be signed upon us.[^3428]

*Verse:* We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord, and we rejoiced and were glad: ... And look upon Thy servants, and upon Thy works, and guide their sons.[^3429]

*On Friday at Matins, Tone 3.*

Through envy of delight I was cast out, having fallen with a grievous fall; but Thou didst not despise me, O Master, having received that which is mine for my sake; Thou art crucified and savest me, and bringest me into glory, O my Redeemer, glory to Thee![^3430]

*Verse:* And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us, and direct Thou the works of our hands upon us; yea, direct Thou the work of our hands.[^3431]

*Martyricon:* Come, all ye people, let us honor the memory of the holy passion-bearers; for having become a spectacle unto angels and unto men, they received crowns of victory from Christ, and pray for our souls.[^3432]

*Glory... both now... Stavrotheotokion:* Beholding Him Who was born of thee, O All-Immaculate One, suspended upon the Tree, thou didst cry out, sobbing: O my sweet child! whither hath Thy light-bearing beauty set, Who hast done good unto the human race?[^3433]

*Then: It is good to give praise: Trisagion. After Our Father: troparion, litany, and the First Hour, and the dismissal.*[^3434]

*At the Liturgy:*
*The Beatitudes, Tone 3.*
*Troparion 1:* Having rejected Thy commandment, O Christ, our forefather Adam didst Thou drive out of paradise; but the thief, O Compassionate One, who confessed Thee on the Cross, didst Thou settle therein, as he cried: Remember me, O Savior, in Thy kingdom.[^3435]

*Troparion 2:* Beholding Thee, the never-setting Sun, upon the Cross, the sun darkened its light, the rocks were split asunder, O Master, all the earth quaked, and the veil of the temple was rent in twain, seeing Thee suffer unjustly, O Savior, Who art incomprehensible unto all.[^3436]

*Troparion 3:* For the sake of all, O Jesus, Life of the living, wast Thou led unto death, that by Thy divine passions Thou mightest save as God those slain by the ancient eating, and reveal them as dwellers of paradise. Therefore with faith we now glorify Thy passions.[^3437]

*Martyricon:* Having been conformed to the passions of Him Who suffered willingly for our sake and took away the reproaches of men, O martyrs, by reason of many torments ye cast down the enemy and obtained the glory on high; therefore are ye glorified in holiness.[^3438]

*Glory...*
*Triadikon:* Unto the Father, and the Son, and the Comforter, the upright Spirit, all we...[^3439]

*On Friday at the Liturgy, Tone 3.*

...faithful, let us bow down, singing in a manner befitting God of one Principle and glory; with pious orthodox understanding let us cry aloud: Remember us in Thy kingdom.[^3440]

*Both now... Stavrotheotokion:* Beholding upon the Cross Her Son suffering in the flesh willingly, the All-Immaculate One was wholly troubled, and weeping, the Pure One cried aloud: Woe is me, O my Child! how art Thou put to death, Who desirest to make alive those grievously dead?[^3441]

## Saturday: All Saints and the Departed[^3442]

*On Friday Evening.*
*On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera to the Holy Martyrs, Hierarchs, and Venerables, Tone 3:*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Great is the power of Thy martyrs" (Ве́лїа мꙋ́ченикъ):*[^3443]

*Sticheron 1:* Suffering stoutly, O valiant martyrs, and having endured wounds and bonds and diverse torments, ye were brought unto an inheritance truly sorrowless and blessed, according to the worthiness of your sufferings.[^3444]

*Sticheron 2:* Having divinely poured forth the word of piety, O hierarchs of the Lord, ye destroyed all heretical contradictions, and were shown forth unto all the faithful as leaders in virtue; therefore are ye honored.[^3445]

*Sticheron 3:* Ye were made like unto the rank of the immaterial and bodiless powers, O God-bearing fathers, showing forth their most glorious life in a material body; therefore do ye dwell in their mansions.[^3446]

*Other Stichera to the Martyrs, same Tone and Podoben (то́йже подо́бенъ):*[^3447]

*Sticheron 4:* Great is the power of Thy Cross, O Lord; for it was set up in a place, yet it works throughout the world, and made Apostles out of fishermen, and Martyrs out of the Gentiles, that they may pray for our souls.[^3448]

*Sticheron 5:* Great is the power of Thy martyrs, O Christ; for lying in their graves they drive away spirits, and abolished the power of the enemy, having contended for piety by faith in the Trinity.[^3449]

*Sticheron 6:* The Prophets, and Apostles of Christ, and the Martyrs, have taught us to sing of the consubstantial Trinity...[^3450]

*On Friday Evening, Tone 3.*

...and enlightened the deceived Gentiles, and made the sons of men partakers with the angels.[^3451]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:* How can we not marvel at thy theandric childbearing, O Most Honored One? For having received no trial of man, O All-Immaculate One, thou didst bear in the flesh a Fatherless Son, Who before all ages was born of the Father without a mother, having in no way undergone change, or commingling, or division, but preserving the property of both essences intact. Therefore, O Mother and Virgin Lady, beseech Him to save the souls of those who in orthodox manner confess thee to be the Theotokos.[^3452]

*O Joyful Light: Prokeimenon of the day.*[^3453]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera for the Martyrs, Tone 3.*[^3454]
*Martyricon:* Thy martyrs, O Lord, having been established by faith, and strengthened by hope, having united themselves in soul by love of Thy Cross, destroyed the tyranny of the enemy and obtained crowns; with the bodiless powers they pray for our souls.[^3455]

*Verse:* Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, unto Thee that dwellest in heaven: ... So our eyes look unto the Lord our God, until He have mercy upon us.[^3456]

*Necrosimon:* All human things are vanity, which abide not after death: riches abide not, nor does glory go down; for when death comes upon them, it destroys all these things. Therefore let us cry aloud unto Christ, the immortal King: Give rest unto those translated from us, where the dwelling of all that rejoice is with Thee.[^3457]

*Verse:* Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us, for we are exceedingly filled with contempt. Our soul is exceedingly filled with the reproach of the prosperous, and with the contempt of the proud.[^3458]

*Necrosimon:* O men, why do we trouble ourselves in vain? The course that we run is short. Life is smoke, vapor and dust and ashes; for a little while it appears, and soon perishes. Therefore let us cry aloud unto Christ, the immortal King: Give rest unto those translated from us, where the dwelling of all that rejoice is with Thee.[^3459]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:* O holy Theotokos among women, unwedded Mother! pray to the King and our God Whom thou hast borne, that He may save us, as He is the Lover of mankind.[^3460]

*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart: Trisagion. After Our Father: troparion and dismissal.*[^3461]

*On Friday at Compline, Tone 3.*

*On Friday at Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Tone 3.*[^3462]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*He Who of old by divine beck gathered the waters together into one gathering, and divided the sea for the people of Israel: this is our God Who is exceedingly glorified; to Him alone let us sing, for He hath been glorified.*[^3463]

*Refrain:* Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.

*Troparion 1:* Having borne beyond all understanding the Author of salvation and the Giver of life, thou didst loose the bond of the condemnation of our first mother Eve. Therefore all creation blesses thee, O pure Mother of God.[^3464]

*Troparion 2:* Snatch me, afflicted by grievous diseases both in soul and body, O most holy Virgin, from the abyss of hopelessness, delivering me from all dire necessities; for thou, O Pure One, art a fountain of tender compassion.[^3465]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* I have stripped off the garments of gladness, having fallen into sorrow and sickness, and am grievously wounded on every side. Anticipate and deliver me from the delusion that holds me fast; for thou, O Lady, art my refuge and hope.[^3466]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* Having acquired thee as a steadfast protectress, O Bride of God, I flee unto thy protection, praying: despise not me thy servant, who am weighed down with many passions and sicknesses and sorrows, but heal me by thy prayers, O Mother of God.[^3467]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*He Who brought all things from nothingness, which are created by the Word and perfected by the Spirit: O Almighty Master, establish me in Thy love.*[^3468]

*Troparion 1:* In perplexity and painful sorrow am I, wretched one, beholding death behind me; therefore, O Pure One, save me by thy prayer.[^3469]

*Troparion 2:* All my life, corrupted by evil and prodigal deeds, accuses me, and plunges me into despair. O Pure One, save me.[^3470]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* The abyss of thy compassions poured forth everywhere, O Lady, for the sake of goodness alone, anoints with rich oil all who ever sing of thee.[^3471]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* As having taken thee from the beginning as the firstfruits of our nature, O Bride of God, Thy Son and Lord gathers together by thy prayers those who sing of thee.[^3472]

*On Friday at Compline, Tone 3.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Thou hast established steadfast love toward us, O Lord; for Thine only-begotten Son didst Thou give unto death for us. Therefore unto Thee we cry, giving thanks: Glory to Thy power, O Lord.*[^3473]

*Troparion 1:* All my hope have I placed upon thee, O Lady; therefore I fall down and pray from my soul: deliver me from deadly disease, leading me up unto the life of salvation, O thou who didst bear the Life.[^3474]

*Troparion 2:* Heal me, O Pure One, by thy prayers, me thy despairing servant, from the likeness of grievous and corrupting sores; for thou didst bear the Physician, our God Who is rich in mercy.[^3475]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Deliver me who have fallen into grievous passions, and grant me the garment of gladness through thy divine visitation, O Virgin who didst bud forth joy that surpasses every mind.[^3476]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* Another heaven wast thou revealed to be, O Theotokos, having ineffably caused the Sun of Righteousness to shine upon the earth, O Pure One, through Whom the co-eternal light of divine knowledge shone forth upon us.[^3477]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Unto Thee I rise early in the morning, the Creator of all, Who art peace surpassing every mind; for Thy commandments are light, wherein guide Thou me.*[^3478]

*Troparion 1:* Causing deadness, I have approached passions from my immeasurable sins; O Mother of God, I fervently pray that thou be the deliverance from my many evils.[^3479]

*Troparion 2:* The hands of my soul do I stretch out unto thee, O Pure One, for in the flesh I, wretched one, have become paralyzed; but deliver me from many wounds, granting healing.[^3480]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* I have stripped off the bright garments, and clothed myself with a mournful robe in sicknesses; unto thee do I pray, O Lady: clothe me with health.[^3481]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* As thou art the sacred comeliness of the martyrs and Apostles, O Theotokos, and the protection of the faithful, we all worthily glorify thee.[^3482]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Lead me up from corruption, O Lord my God, Jonah cried aloud; and I cry unto Thee: deliver me, O Savior, from the depth of my many evils, and guide me, I pray, unto Thy light.*[^3483]

*Troparion 1:* The corrupter hath bent against me a multitude of diseases from my immeasurable transgressions, and with deadening...[^3484]

*On Friday at Compline, Tone 3.*

...they have encompassed me; but do thou thyself, O Theotokos, deliver me from these, and snatch me from every necessity.[^3485]

*Troparion 2:* I was cast down into the depth of transgressions and sicknesses and immeasurable sorrows, and in these am I now utterly choked; but do thou, O Mother of God, stretch out thy hand, and lead me up unto salvation.[^3486]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Thee do I know as a firm foundation, O Theotokos, and a mighty help unto those who are in sorrow; therefore, having fled unto thy protection, I pray thee: deliver me from grievous tribulations and from heavy sicknesses.[^3487]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* Let us sing of the furrow that budded forth the divine ear of corn, whereby the hearts of the faithful are nourished by faith, and noetic famine hath ceased, the Ark of God, as the Virgin Mother of God.[^3488]

*Lord, have mercy, thrice. Glory... both now...*
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*
Without departing from the divine nature, He became flesh in thy womb; but having become man, He remained God: He Who after childbirth preserved thee a Virgin Mother, as before childbirth, all-immaculate, the only Lord. Beseech Him fervently to grant us great mercy.[^3489]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*As of old Thou didst bedew the three pious children in the Chaldean flame with the bright fire of the Godhead: illuminate us also who cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.*[^3490]

*Troparion 1:* Have mercy upon me, O Virgin Bride of God, who have slipped into the depth of despair; I pray that thou be stillness unto me, O Pure One; for thou art ever a haven of salvation unto those in distress.[^3491]

*Troparion 2:* Ineffably beyond understanding didst thou bear Christ, the Wisdom and Power of God; therefore, being mighty in mercy and compassionate, grant me thy saving healings, O Theotokos, hope of the faithful.[^3492]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Having enriched the ends of the earth with the sweetness of incorruption by thy divine childbearing, liberate me from corrupting disease and bitter passions, and grant salvation, O all-glorious Mother of God.[^3493]

*On Friday at Compline, Tone 3.*

*Both now... Theotokion:* He Who set thee before Himself as a mystical door, and a marvelous protectress unto those on earth, through thee, O Pure One, granted a door unto men, leading all into eternal life, as He is compassionate and the God of all.[^3494]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The Babylonian furnace burned not the children, nor did the fire of the Godhead consume the Virgin; therefore with the children let us the faithful cry aloud: Bless the Lord, ye works of the Lord.*[^3495]

*Troparion 1:* Having acquired an unsearchable deep of transgressions, I am now being choked, O Pure One, plunged into sicknesses; therefore, stretching out a hand of help unto me, lead me up from the depth of sicknesses, I pray.[^3496]

*Troparion 2:* An earthly throne unto God and a heavenly door wast thou revealed to be, O pure Ever-Virgin, all-lauded Theotokos. Therefore open unto me the door of salvation, O Mother of God, and liberate me from the darkness of passions.[^3497]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Being the vine, thou didst sprout an untended cluster, O Pure One, pouring forth wine for souls, that gladdens those who faithfully sing of Him, O All-Immaculate One.[^3498]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* Having remained incorrupt after childbirth, O All-Immaculate One, we pray thee, O Pure One: deliver from corruption thy servants, who faithfully sing with one accord of soul: Bless all the works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.[^3499]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*On Mount Sinai Moses saw thee in the bush, having unconsumed conceived in thy womb the fire of the Godhead; and Daniel saw thee as an unquarried mountain; Isaiah cried aloud of a rod that budded from the root of David.*[^3500]

*Troparion 1:* O good Virgin! O joy and refuge of the world! O storehouse of the faithful, and deliverance from sorrows! do thou stand by me at the hour of death, and deliver me from demons seeking to corrupt me.[^3501]

*Troparion 2:* Having conceived the Creator and God in thy womb, O pure Virgin Mother, by the power that is in thee cast down the insolence of demons, and exalt the horn of those who sing of thy divine dominion and faithfully bow down before thine icon.[^3502]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Having made our nature new, He Who was incarnate of thee, O pure Virgin, makes new and renews through thy motherly prayer those who unceasingly magnify thee in hymns as their bright and vigilant protectress, and the true Theotokos.[^3503]

*On Friday at Compline, Tone 3.*

*Both now... Theotokion:* Thou art the joy of the angels, thou art the beauty of the righteous, thou art the hope of the faithful, thou art our preservation, thou art the bridge that translates unto unaging life those who magnify thee with faith and love.[^3504]

*Then: It is truly meet: Trisagion. After Our Father: troparia, and the rest as usual, and dismissal.*[^3505]

*On Saturday at Matins.*
*After the first Kathisma reading, Sessional Hymns for the Martyrs, Tone 3.*[^3506]
*Martyricon 1:* The stout-heartedness of your patience vanquished the wiles of the evil-originating foe, O all-lauded passion-bearers! Therefore were ye accounted worthy of eternal blessedness. But pray to the Lord to save the flock of the Christ-loving people, being witnesses of the truth.[^3507]

*Verse:* God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel.[^3508]

*Martyricon 2:* Ye shine forth by faith as most radiant luminaries, O holy ones, physicians of the sick, O most lauded passion-bearers! For ye feared not the wounds of the tormentors, and cast down idolatrous impiety, having an unconquerable victory, the true Cross.[^3509]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:* Thee who didst intercede for the salvation of our race do we hymn, O Virgin Theotokos; for having taken flesh from thee, Thy Son and our God, through the Cross having received passion, delivered us from corruption, as He is the Lover of mankind.[^3510]

*After the second Kathisma reading, Sessional Hymns, Tone 3.*[^3511]
*Martyricon 1:* Having armed yourselves with the whole armor of Christ, and having clothed yourselves in the weapon of faith, ye cast down the hostile ranks in martyrdom; for zealously, in hope of life, ye endured all the threats and wounds of the tormentors. Therefore also ye received crowns, O patient-minded martyrs of Christ.[^3512]

*Verse:* God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel.[^3513]

*Martyricon 2:* O holy passion-bearers, pray to the merciful God, that He may grant remission of transgressions unto our souls.[^3514]

*Verse:* Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken to Thyself, O Lord.[^3515]

*Necrosimon:* When by Thy word we shall stand before Thine unbribable judgment-seat, O Lord, put not to shame those who have believed in Thee, O our Savior; for we all have sinned, but from Thee...[^3516]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 3.*

...have we not departed. Therefore we pray Thee: in the mansions of Thy righteous ones, O Christ, give rest unto those whom Thou hast taken to Thyself; for Thou alone art without sin.[^3517]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:* The Prophets proclaimed, the Apostles taught, the Martyrs confessed, and we have believed that thou art truly the Theotokos; therefore we also magnify thy childbearing, O Pure One.[^3518]

*Canon to the Holy Martyrs, Hierarchs, Venerables, and for the Departed. (A composition of Joseph.) Tone 3.*[^3519]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Marvelous in glory, Who doest wonders art Thou, O God, Who didst turn the deep into dry land, and didst drown the chariots, and didst save the people who sing unto Thee, as our King and God.*[^3520]

*Martyricon:* The glory of martyrdom did ye find, O all-wise ones, all ye sufferers, having valiantly endured diverse torments; therefore are ye ever glorified.[^3521]

*To the Hierarchs:* Possessing a life on high, ye were shown forth as divine ministers, all-wise initiates of the mystery of the incarnate Word, having well tended the Lord's flock by divine power.[^3522]

*To the Venerables:* Having crucified yourselves unto the world, O Venerable ones, ye cast away all fleshly pleasures, and were shown forth as vessels of the Spirit, manifestly destroying seductive spirits by divine power.[^3523]

*Glory...*
*Necrosimon:* Those who from the earth by Thy command, O Lord, have been translated with pious faith, O Compassionate One, having accounted them worthy of endless life and never-waning light, give them rest, as Thou art good.[^3524]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* As having borne God in the flesh, O Pure One, with pious disposition we unceasingly honor thee, together with the honored women who shone forth in fasting, and cast down the enemy by their sufferings.[^3525]

*Another Canon for the Departed, chanted in succession when there is no Menaion. (A composition of Theophanes.)*[^3526]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Passing through the sea divided by the rod of old, Israel passed as through a wilderness; for it revealed a figure of the Cross, preparing paths in advance. Therefore we sing in praise unto our marvelous God, for He hath been glorified.*[^3527]

*Refrain:* God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel.[^3528]

*Troparion 1:* Having slain death by Thy burial, Thou didst cast down the tyranny of hades...[^3529]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 3.*

...having ascended as our Forerunner into heaven, Thou didst bear up with Thyself the choir of passion-bearers; and now give rest, O Christ, unto the souls translated unto Thee.[^3530]

*Refrain:* Give rest, O Lord, to the souls of Thy departed servants.[^3531]

*Troparion 2:* O Thou Who strengthenest the divine martyrs, and by them didst destroy delusion: by their supplications as God, be Thou pleased, O Savior, that those who have reposed in Thee obtain immortality and a blessed inheritance.[^3532]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* For Thy servants, O Master, having been slain Thou didst pour out Thy blood as Thou art compassionate, paying the debt for them; therefore, O Merciful One, we pray Thee: give rest unto Thy servants translated unto Thee.[^3533]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* Let us hymn the pure Theotokos, who bore God, Who by His death overthrew our death, and poured forth unaging life and blessedness that abides unto the ages.[^3534]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*O barren and childless soul, acquire glorious fruit, and rejoicing cry aloud: I am established through Thee, O God! There is none holy, there is none righteous beside Thee, O Lord.*[^3535]

*Martyricon:* Strengthened by the power of God, O glorious martyrs, ye utterly destroyed the ruin-bearing power of the enemy who was strong in malice, and received divine crowns of victory.[^3536]

*To the Venerables and Hierarchs:* O Master Who didst reveal the Venerable ones as victors over the enemy, and Who by most holy anointing didst sanctify the Hierarchs, O Christ: by their supplications sanctify and enlighten those who sing to Thee.[^3537]

*To the Holy Women:* Together with all the Prophets, let us praise the divine choir of women, who having shone forth in fasting, and having suffered according to the faith, trampled down the manifoldly crafty serpent.[^3538]

*Glory...*
*Necrosimon:* Those whom Thou hast translated from us by Thy divine will, O Christ, rank Thou with the saints, having overlooked their transgressions in life, O Compassionate One, we pray, by the supplications of Thy saints.[^3539]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* We who are saved by thy holy childbearing, O Pure One, cry aloud unto thee with faith Gabriel's greeting: Rejoice! and we pray: by thy prayers ask forgiveness of transgressions for us all.[^3540]

*Another Heirmos:*
*He Who brought all things from nothingness, which are created by the Word, perfected...*[^3541]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 3.*

...by the Spirit, O Almighty Master: establish me in Thy love.[^3542]

*Necrosimon:* O Thou Who didst create me from the earth, and when I, wretched, was returned unto the earth, didst renew me more brightly: give rest unto the souls of the departed, by the supplications of the martyrs.[^3543]

*Martyricon:* Being entreated by the beatings and bonds, the wounds and stripes of the passion-bearing martyrs: settle Thou the souls of Thy servants in the holy habitations, O Compassionate One.[^3544]

*Glory...*
*Necrosimon:* Deliver Thy servants from Gehenna, O Lover of mankind, and from the fire and dark habitations of hades, who in faith, O Master, have fallen asleep in hope of Thee.[^3545]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* O most holy Lady, as being the Mother of the Creator of creation, Who destroyed the power of death, O all-pure Theotokos, and granted us incorruption.[^3546]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Habakkuk foresaw the overshadowing mountain, even thine all-pure womb, O Pure One; therefore he cried aloud: God shall come from Theman, and the Holy One from a mountain overshadowed and densely wooded.*[^3547]

*Martyricon:* As sheep were ye all led, having willingly been slaughtered as lambs, O wise sufferers, unto the Lamb, the Word of God, Who was well pleased to be slain upon the tree of the Cross for the human race.[^3548]

*To the Hierarchs and Venerables:* With the Venerables let us honor the glorious high priests, who were luminaries unto the faithful, who destroyed heresies and the deep darkness of the passions, and passed over by faith unto the unwaning Light.[^3549]

*To the Prophets and Holy Women:* By the beauty of their words the divinely-speaking Prophets enlighten the souls of the faithful; and by the splendors of their ascetical struggles and the dawning of their wonders, the God-bearing women illumine their hearts.[^3550]

*Necrosimon. Glory...*
Show forth those who have left this life to be partakers of transcendent glory, O Word of God, granting them, O Good One, deliverance from the transgressions which they committed on earth in knowledge and in ignorance.[^3551]

*Theotokion:* Let us hymn the all-praised Mother of the Lord: the glory of the martyrs, the divine adornment of hierarchs and venerables, the confirmation of the faithful, and the preaching of the prophets.[^3552]

*Another Heirmos:*
*Thou hast established a steadfast love toward us, O Lord; for Thou didst deliver Thine Only-begotten Son unto death for us. Therefore we cry unto Thee in thanksgiving: Glory to Thy power, O Lord.*[^3553]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 3.*

*Necrosimon:* Having mercifully taken up Thy dwelling in the grave for us, O Master, Thou didst empty the graves as God; and having shown forth the martyrs as victorious, settle Thou Thy departed servants in the place of refreshment.[^3554]

*Martyricon:* O Thou Who rejoicing acceptest the valiant contests, the wounds, and the severing of limbs of the sufferers which they endured for Thy sake, O Master: deliver from torment those whom Thou hast taken to Thyself.[^3555]

*Glory...*
*Necrosimon:* O Thou Who by Thy nod holdest the measure of life: vouchsafe Thine endless life and incorruptible glory unto those whom Thou hast translated unto Thyself, having destroyed the dividing wall of sins.[^3556]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* A Mother in truth and a Virgin wast thou shown forth, O All-Immaculate One, who alone didst truly unite conception and childbirth with virginity: for thou didst bear God Who destroyed the power of death.[^3557]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*With Thine unwaning light, O Christ God, enlighten my humble soul, and guide me in Thy fear; for Thy commandments are light.*[^3558]

*Martyricon:* Your wounds and stripes were shown to be incurable wounds unto the enemy; but now they heal the stripes of all the faithful, O sufferers of the Lord.[^3559]

*To the Venerables, Hierarchs, and Prophets:* Let us sing of the multitude of the Venerables, and bless the Hierarchs of Christ, and honor His Prophets, who now ever pray for us.[^3560]

*To the Holy Women:* Loving God Who became incarnate for our sake, the all-glorious women, having suffered with an upright disposition and having fasted, now abide in the heavens.[^3561]

*Necrosimon. Glory...*
In the delight of Paradise, O Lord, in the land of the living, O Christ, where Thy light shines, settle Thy faithful servants whom Thou hast translated from the earth.[^3562]

*Theotokion:* From thee, O Pure One, God was incarnate, and hath now shown thee forth higher than the Angels, and excelling all creatures, O Lady; therefore we sing unto thee.[^3563]

*Another Heirmos:*
*Unto Thee I rise early, the Creator of all, Who passest all the understanding of the world; for Thy commandments are light, wherein do Thou guide me.*[^3564]

*Necrosimon:* In the church of the firstborn, O Good One, those who have passed away from us...[^3565]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 3.*

...by the supplications of the martyrs and honored sufferers, number Thou with the righteous.[^3566]

*Necrosimon:* For the deliverance from many transgressions Thou didst pour out Thy most holy blood, O Christ; and now by the supplications of Thy martyrs, O Savior, give rest unto those who have fallen asleep in piety.[^3567]

*Glory...*
*Necrosimon:* Direct the souls of the departed, O Word, into the eternal place of Thy delight, which is beyond understanding, and vouchsafe unto them the divine splendor of the saints.[^3568]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* Thee, O Virgin, who beyond word didst conceive in thy womb the uncontainable Word, Who gives life unto the dead, do we worthily bless.[^3569]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*The depth of the passions hath risen up against me, and a tempest of contrary winds; but do Thou anticipate me and save me, O Savior, and deliver me from corruption, as Thou didst save the Prophet from the sea monster.*[^3570]

*Martyricon:* Let the luminaries of the honored Church, the most holy lambs of the Lamb and Shepherd, the all-glorious sufferers of Christ, be honored with sacred hymns.[^3571]

*To the Venerables and Hierarchs:* The assembly of the Venerables was exalted, having exalted God in humility; and the multitude of Hierarchs was glorified in good works, having glorified the Holy Trinity.[^3572]

*To the Holy Women:* By the displays of divine wonders, and by perfect endurance of afflictions, having valiantly fought like men, the sacred choir of women wounded the iniquitous foes.[^3573]

*Necrosimon. Glory...*
Those whom Thou hast received from the earth in faith, number Thou with the assemblies of the saints, and settle them in the bosom of faithful Abraham, O Christ, ever to glorify Thy manifold tenderheartedness.[^3574]

*Theotokion:* Thou art the praise of martyrs, prophets, and venerables, and of all the righteous from the ages, O Pure One! Therefore with brightly joyful voices we honor thee together with them, O Theotokos.[^3575]

*Another Heirmos:*
*Lead me up from corruption, O Lord my God, Jonah cried aloud; and I cry unto Thee: deliver me, O Savior, from the depth of my many evils, and guide me, I pray, unto Thy light.*[^3576]

*Necrosimon:* Turn the weeping of those who have fallen asleep in faith into joy, by the supplications of the passion-bearers, girding them comely with gladness, O Christ, and directing and guiding them unto Thy light.[^3577]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 3.*

*Necrosimon:* Give rest, O God, according to the multitude of Thy compassions, in the bosom of the Patriarchs unto those who have been translated, where the bright light of Thy countenance shines, O Christ, overlooking all their transgressions.[^3578]

*Glory...*
*Necrosimon:* Make marvelous Thy wonderful mercy of Thy love for mankind, O Christ, upon those who have departed hence and been delivered from the grievous things of life, and fill them with Thy joy and meekness.[^3579]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* From deadness and corruption and death were we delivered by thy most glorious childbirth, O Mother of God; for thou, O pure one, didst bear for us the Fountain of incorruption, and with thy light didst enlighten all the world.[^3580]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The three children, prefiguring the Trinity in the furnace, trampled upon the fiery threat, and singing cried aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers.*[^3581]

*Martyricon:* Standing in the midst of the fire, O all-glorious passion-bearers of the Lord, ye received the divine dew from heaven; and having been put to death by torments, ye made the manifoldly wily enemy a corpse.[^3582]

*To the Hierarchs:* The divine Hierarchs, having steered the Church of Christ as a ship, preserved it unsubmerged, having in truth escaped the evil waves of delusion.[^3583]

*To the Venerables:* Having slain the flesh by sacred ascetical struggles, O true fasters, ye inherited the unaging life of dispassion, singing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers.[^3584]

*Glory...*
*Necrosimon:* Show not Thy faithful servants whom Thou hast translated to be guilty at the judgment; but give them rest, O Word, in the splendor of Thy saints, to sing of Thy tenderheartedness.[^3585]

*Theotokion:* Of martyrs and venerables and prophets, O All-Immaculate One, and of holy women, and of all high priests who lived in holiness, thou art the glory in truth; and with them we honor thee.[^3586]

*Another Heirmos:*
*As of old Thou didst bedew the three pious children in the Chaldean flame with the bright fire of the Godhead: illuminate us also who cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.*[^3587]

*Necrosimon:* O Word of God, Who art the life-originating cause and creative power unto all: vouchsafe the delight promised unto the martyrs unto the souls of the translated servants of...[^3588]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 3.*

...Thine, who cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.[^3589]

*Martyricon:* For their bodies as corruptible the glorious martyrs valiantly cared not, O Christ; and now with boldness they pray unto Thee: give rest unto the souls of Thy translated servants, O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou.[^3590]

*Glory...*
*Necrosimon:* With the reed of the Cross Thou didst sign forgiveness of sins for all the faithful; vouchsafe now that the souls whom Thou hast translated unto Thyself may partake thereof, and sing unto Thee with gladness: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou.[^3591]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* The Word of the Father, Who makes all things by His will, as God renewed human nature buried by the passions; blessed, O Pure One, is the fruit of thy womb.[^3592]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*God Who is unceasingly glorified on high by the Angels: ye heavens of heavens, earth, and mountains, and hills, and the deep, and all the human race: bless Him with hymns as Creator and Deliverer, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.*[^3593]

*Martyricon:* Severely encompassed by the infliction of stripes and by the assaults of afflictions, ye did not deny true life, O passion-bearers of the Lord, nor did ye give honors unto graven images, unto deceitful delusion.[^3594]

*To the Martyrs:* Placed as luminaries upon the lampstands of honorable virtues, ye enlightened the souls of all, having driven away all darkness, O sacredly-working passion-bearers, co-dwellers with the heavenly minds.[^3595]

*To the Prophets, Venerables, and Holy Women:* Let the praiseworthy Prophets, and the company of the Venerables, and the glorious multitude of all the pious women be worthily hymned, who pray for us unto the Savior and God.[^3596]

*Necrosimon. Glory...*
O Thou Who rulest over the living: settle Thy faithful ones whom Thou hast translated from the earth with the saints in the light of Thy countenance, granting them forgiveness of transgressions for the sake of Thy great mercy, O Savior.[^3597]

*Theotokion:* Let the much-hymned Virgin, the preaching of the prophets, the adornment of hierarchs, sufferers, and venerables, and the joy of holy women, be sung as is meet unto the ages.[^3598]

*Another Heirmos:*
*Enduring to serve the living God in Babylon, the children cared not for musical instruments, and...*[^3599]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 3.*

...standing in the midst of the flame, they sang a divinely-fitting hymn, saying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.[^3600]

*Necrosimon:* Being the Treasure of immortality, Thou, O Author of life, didst proclaim incorruption unto the dead, which Thou didst grant unto Thy martyrs, O Christ, who sing unto Thee with pious faith: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.[^3601]

*Martyricon:* By spiritual wisdom and the endurance of deeds, the steadfast sufferers, having cared not for torments, ask of Christ forgiveness for the departed souls, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.[^3602]

*Glory...*
*Necrosimon:* Cleanse those who have been translated unto Thee, O Good One, having torn up their handwriting by the spear of Thy side; and having taken away the dividing wall of their transgressions, be Thou pleased, O Savior, to receive them to hymn Thee: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.[^3603]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* We know thee to be a noetic cloud unto those overwhelmed by the thirst of mortification, O pure Virgin, pouring forth the living water of forgiveness, and granting immortality unto all the dead who ever cry in faith: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.[^3604]

*Then: My soul doth magnify the Lord.*[^3605]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*On Mount Sinai Moses saw thee in the bush, having unconsumed conceived in thy womb the fire of the Godhead; and Daniel saw thee as an unquarried mountain; Isaiah cried aloud of a rod that budded from the root of David.*[^3606]

*Martyricon:* As sheep were ye led unto Him Who was slaughtered for our sake, and ye filled the divine choirs of Angels with joy, O passion-bearers of Christ. Therefore by your prayers establish all, and deliver all from the harmful delusion of the enemy.[^3607]

*To the Hierarchs and Venerables:* Having the word of life as luminaries, ye enlightened souls, O most holy high priests of Christ, partakers of divine glory; and having received the Spirit as fire, O Venerable ones, ye consumed the passions and destroyed the kindling of idols.[^3608]

*To the Prophets, Venerables, and Holy Women:* With the holy Prophets let us honor the multitude of the Venerables who before the Law and under the Law shone forth by an honorable life, and let us praise the choirs of holy women, and cry aloud: O Lord, by their prayers save us all.[^3609]

*Glory...*
*Necrosimon:* Thy burial and Resurrection, O Christ, became life unto all. For the sake of this...[^3610]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 3.*

...taking courage we cry unto Thee: with all the chosen, give rest unto Thy faithful servants whom Thou hast received, forgiving all their transgressions, as Thou art the all-good God.[^3611]

*Theotokion:* Fearful is this place! cried Jacob, when he truly saw the prefiguring of thee in the ladder, O Theotokos, glory of martyrs and boast of venerables, adornment of Angels, and salvation of all the Prophets and of the faithful.[^3612]

*Another Heirmos:*
*Her who was prefigured by the bush and the fire on Sinai unto Moses the lawgiver, and who unconsumed conceived the fire of the Godhead in her womb, the all-radiant and unquenchable Lamp, the true Theotokos, honoring her with hymns do we magnify.*[^3613]

*Necrosimon:* Now as the only good God and Lover of mankind, settle those who have been translated unto Thee in the land of the meek, by the supplications of the martyrs, granting them remission of transgressions, O Merciful One, that unceasingly with hymns we may magnify Thee.[^3614]

*Necrosimon:* In the holy habitations, in the bosom of Abraham, O Christ, number those whom Thou hast received with Thy righteous ones, where shines the ineffable and divine light of Thy countenance, and rejoicing that abides in truth unto the ages, ever-existing.[^3615]

*Glory...*
*Necrosimon:* Of Thy blessed life and the unceasing food of eternal goods, and of true gladness, vouchsafe Thy servants whom Thou hast translated by Thy will, O Giver of life, in a place of pasture, beside the waters of rest.[^3616]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* As a holy ark and tabernacle of testimony of God the Lawgiver, thou didst receive in thy womb, O All-Immaculate One, thy Creator, Who by His death overthrew the decree of the ancient curse and the law of death.[^3617]

*It is truly meet: Litany, Exapostilarion, and the usual psalms.*[^3618]

*At the Praises, Stichera of the Martyrs, Tone 3.*[^3619]
*Martyricon 1:* The warriors of Christ cast away the fear of kings and torturers, and with goodly boldness and courage confessed Him, the Lord God of all and our King, and they pray for our souls.[^3620]

*Martyricon 2:* The powers of the holy Angels were astonished at the sufferings of the martyrs; for clothed in mortal flesh, they cared not for torments, having become imitators of the passions...[^3621]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 3.*

...of the Savior Christ; and they pray for our souls.[^3622]

*Martyricon 3:* As luminaries in the world ye shine even after death, O holy martyrs, having fought the good fight; and having boldness, entreat Christ that He have mercy upon our souls.[^3623]

*Necrosimon:* Ye men, why are we troubled in vain? The course wherein we run is short; life is smoke, vapor, dust, and ashes: it appears for a little while, and quickly perishes. Therefore let us cry unto Christ the Immortal King: Give rest unto those translated from us, where the dwelling of all who rejoice is with Thee.[^3624]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:*
Without seed didst thou conceive of the Holy Spirit, and glorifying we sing unto thee: Rejoice, O most holy Virgin![^3625]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera for the Departed.*
*(A composition of Theophanes.) Tone 3.*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Great is the power of Thy Cross, O Lord" (Ве́лїа крⷭ҇та̀ твоегѡ̀):*
*Sticheron 1:* I glorify Thy precious Cross, whereby life and the enjoyment of delight are bestowed upon those who sing unto Thee with faith and love, O only Plenteous in mercy! Therefore we cry unto Thee, O Christ God: Give rest unto those who have departed from us, where the dwelling of all who rejoice is with Thee.[^3626]

*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken to Thyself, O Lord.*[^3627]

*Sticheron 2:* O only Merciful and Tenderhearted One, Who hast an incomprehensible abyss of goodness, Who knowest human nature which Thou didst fashion: we pray Thee, O Christ God: Give rest unto those translated from us, where the dwelling of all who rejoice is with Thee.[^3628]

*Verse: Their souls shall dwell among good things.*[^3629]

*Sticheron 3:* Thou didst fall asleep in the grave as man, but by Thine invincible power as God Thou didst raise up those sleeping in the graves, who offer unceasing hymns unto Thee. Therefore we pray Thee, O Christ God: Give rest unto those translated from us, where the dwelling of all who rejoice is with Thee.[^3630]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:*
We all know thee, O Theotokos, to be a noetic lamp bearing the Light of the Godhead united unto the coarseness of human nature; entreat thy Son and God to give rest unto those translated from us, where...[^3631]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 3.*

...the rejoicing of all who rejoice is found.[^3632]

*Then: It is good to give thanks: Trisagion. After Our Father: troparion, Litany, and dismissal. Then the First Hour.*[^3633]

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes, Tone 3.*[^3634]
*Troparion 1:* Adam our forefather, who rejected Thy commandment, O Christ, Thou didst cast out of Paradise; but the thief who confessed Thee upon the Cross, O Compassionate One, Thou didst settle therein, as he cried: Remember me, O Savior, in Thy kingdom.[^3635]

*Martyricon:* Mingling yourselves with fiery wounds, O passion-bearers of Christ, ye found heavenly dew cooling and strengthening you to endure the bitter bodily pains; therefore ye ever lighten every disease from our souls.[^3636]

*To the All-Saints:* The sacred Hierarchs, the glorious Prophets, the multitude of the Venerables, and the assembly of divine women who suffered in faith and cast down the enemy's delusion, obtained heavenly glory; by whose supplications, O Savior, have compassion upon Thy servants.[^3637]

*Necrosimon:* Where Thy light shines, in the mansions of Thine elect, O God, where ever-existing food is, settle those who have been translated from us in faith, O Jesus, overlooking their transgressions, O Savior, that we may fervently glorify Thee, O Master.[^3638]

*Glory... Triadikon:*
Unto the Father, Son, and divine Spirit let us the faithful bow down, unto the uncreated Trinity in three Persons and in one Godhead, Who is ever faithfully glorified by the bodiless hosts in threefold sacred doxologies.[^3639]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Enlightenment and cleansing, the Deliverer of all appeared from thee, O Lady full of divine grace. Fervently entreat Him, at the coming fearful judgment, to deliver us from all condemnation, O Pure One, who ever faithfully sing unto thee.[^3640]

---

### Notes

[^2263]: First Resurrectional Sticheron at "Lord, I have cried" of Tone 3 Little Vespers, chanted twice according to the rubric `повторѧ́юще пе́рвꙋю`.
[^2264]: Second Resurrectional Sticheron at Little Vespers, celebrating the reopening of Paradise through the Resurrection.
[^2265]: Third Sticheron at Little Vespers, a Trinitarian doxology confessing the consubstantial and uncreated Trinity.
[^2266]: Dogmatic Theotokion of Little Vespers, Tone 3, contemplating the ineffable wonder of the Incarnation from the virgin daughter of Adam.
[^2267]: Rubric for Tone 3 Little Vespers: O Gladsome Light (*Phos hilaron*), Saturday Prokeimenon (Psalm 92:1 [LXX]), and "Vouchsafe, O Lord," omitting the ektenia.
[^2268]: First Aposticha Resurrectional Sticheron of Little Vespers, Tone 3, recalling the darkening of the sun during the Crucifixion (Luke 23:44-45).
[^2269]: Psalm 44:15 [LXX].
[^2270]: First Theotokion Sticheron at the Aposticha of Little Vespers, depicting the Virgin as a paradise river of divine gifts (Genesis 2:10).
[^2271]: Psalm 44:11 [LXX].
[^2272]: Second Theotokion Sticheron at the Aposticha, gathering Old Testament types: the tabernacle (*skēnē*), the mountain uncut by hands (Daniel 2:34), the golden urn of manna (Hebrews 9:4), and the Ark of the Covenant.
[^2273]: Psalm 44:18 [LXX].
[^2274]: Penitential troparion at the Aposticha, praying for repentance and a place on the right hand at the Last Judgment (Matthew 25:33-34).
[^2275]: Doxasticon Theotokion concluding Little Vespers Aposticha, honoring the Virgin through whom recreation and resurrection were granted to humanity.
[^2276]: Rubric concluding Tone 3 Little Vespers: Canticle of Symeon (*Nunc Dimittis*), Trisagion, Resurrectional Troparion, Theotokion, Augmented Litany, and Dismissal.
[^2277]: First Resurrectional Sticheron at "Lord, I have cried" of Tone 3 Great Vespers (repeated from Little Vespers).
[^2278]: Second Resurrectional Sticheron at Great Vespers.
[^2279]: Third Resurrectional Sticheron at Great Vespers.
[^2280]: First Anatolian Sticheron (Sticheron 4 at "Lord, I have cried"), citing Isaiah 53:5 ("with His stripes we are healed").
[^2281]: Second Anatolian Sticheron (Sticheron 5 at "Lord, I have cried").
[^2282]: Third Anatolian Sticheron (Sticheron 6 at "Lord, I have cried"), echoing John 16:33 ("be of good cheer; I have overcome the world") and John 11:25 ("I am the resurrection and the life").
[^2283]: Fourth Anatolian Sticheron (Sticheron 7 at "Lord, I have cried"), Psalm 129:1 [LXX] ("Out of the depths have I cried unto Thee, O Lord").
[^2284]: Opening of the great Tone 3 Dogmatic Theotokion at Great Vespers by St. John of Damascus, continued on physical leaf p259.
[^2285]: Conclusion of the Tone 3 Dogmatic Theotokion, continued from physical leaf p258, enshrining the Chalcedonian Christological definition ("without confusion, without change, without division, without separation").
[^2286]: Rubric for Great Vespers: Entrance with the censer, *Phos hilaron*, Saturday Prokeimenon, and the Litanies.
[^2287]: First Aposticha Resurrectional Sticheron of Tone 3 Great Vespers.
[^2288]: Psalm 92:1 [LXX].
[^2289]: Second Aposticha Sticheron (first alphabetical sticheron, letter *Zeta* / *Zhivopriyemnoye*), celebrating deliverance from Adam's ancestral curse.
[^2290]: Psalm 92:1 [LXX].
[^2291]: Third Aposticha Sticheron, meditating upon the unchangeable God suffering impassibly in the flesh.
[^2292]: Psalm 92:5 [LXX].
[^2293]: Fourth Aposticha Sticheron, confessing Christ's Resurrection raising all who believe.
[^2294]: Aposticha Theotokion of Tone 3 Great Vespers, confessing Christ begotten of the Father without mother and born of the Virgin without father.
[^2295]: Opening of the canonical Sunday Resurrectional Troparion of Tone 3 (*Da veselyatsia nebesnaya*), continued on physical leaf p260.
[^2296]: Conclusion of the Sunday Resurrectional Troparion of Tone 3, continued from physical leaf p259, alluding to Psalm 97:1 [LXX] and Colossians 1:18 ("firstborn from the dead"). The Slavonic text contains a physical typographical error: *пе́рбвенецъ* for *пе́рвенецъ*.
[^2297]: Resurrectional Dismissal Theotokion of Tone 3 (*Tyat khodataystvovavshuyu*), chanted throughout the Octoechos cycle.
[^2298]: Heirmos of Ode 1 (Saturday Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 3), based on the First Canticle of Moses (Exodus 14:21-31, 15:1).
[^2299]: First troparion of Ode 1 (Compline Canon), greeting the Virgin with the Archangel Gabriel's salutation (Luke 1:28).
[^2300]: Second troparion of Ode 1 (Compline Canon), contrasting Eve's creation from Adam's rib (Genesis 2:21-22) with Christ's incarnation from the Virgin's womb.
[^2301]: Third troparion (`Сла́ва:`) of Ode 1 (Compline Canon), referencing the primal sentence upon Eve (Genesis 3:16) abolished by the seedless birth of Christ.
[^2302]: Concluding Theotokion (`И҆ ны́нѣ:`) of Ode 1 (Compline Canon), fleeing to the Mother of God from the abyss of despair.
[^2303]: Heirmos of Ode 3 (Compline Canon), confessing creation *ex nihilo* by the Father through the Word and the Holy Spirit (Psalm 32:6 [LXX]).
[^2304]: First troparion of Ode 3 (Compline Canon), interpreting Aaron's budding rod (Numbers 17:8) as a prophetic figure of the Virgin's virginal childbearing.
[^2305]: Opening of Troparion 2 of Ode 3 (Compline Canon), continued on physical leaf p261, portraying the Virgin as a golden lamp-stand bearing the divine Fire.
[^2306]: The troparion completing Ode 3 of the Compline Theotokion canon begins on leaf p260 and concludes on physical leaf p261 (printed fol. 254).
[^2307]: Saturday Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion at Glory.
[^2308]: In the 1895 Stauropegion edition, the rubric 'Both now...' (*И҆ ны́нѣ:*) is omitted in red print before this troparion, functioning as the concluding Theotokion of Ode 3.
[^2309]: Ode 4 Heirmos from the Heirmologion (Tone 3), based on Habakkuk 3:2-3.
[^2310]: Habakkuk 3:3: 'God will come from Teman [the South], and the Holy One from mount Paran.'
[^2311]: Daniel 2:34-35, 45 (the stone cut out of the mountain without hands).
[^2312]: Psalm 67:16-17 [LXX 67:16, 'A mountain of God, a rich mountain... God is well pleased to dwell in it'].
[^2313]: Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 4, concluding Theotokion.
[^2314]: Isaiah 6:1-5 (Isaiah's vision of the Lord of Hosts on the high throne).
[^2315]: Isaiah 11:1, 10 (the rod of Jesse and the Root of Jesse in Whom the Gentiles trust).
[^2316]: Isaiah 9:6-7 (the Prince of Peace). The troparion spans physical leaves p261 and p262.
[^2317]: Numbers 24:17 ('A sceptre shall rise out of Israel, and shall smite the princes of Moab').
[^2318]: Isaiah 19:1 ('Behold, the Lord rids upon a light cloud, and shall come into Egypt').
[^2319]: Dogmatic confession of Christ in two natures, perfect God and perfect man (Fourth and Sixth Ecumenical Councils).
[^2320]: Matthew 14:30-31 (Christ saving Peter from sinking in the storm).
[^2321]: Song of Songs 3:7 ('Behold his bed, which is Solomon's').
[^2322]: Patristic imagery of Christ assuming humanity as royal purple from the Virgin's pure blood.
[^2323]: Baruch 3:37 [LXX 3:38]: 'Afterward did He show Himself upon earth, and conversed with men.'
[^2324]: Anti-iconoclastic hymnographic witness defending the veneration of the holy icons of the Theotokos against heretics.
[^2325]: Sessional Hymn following Ode 6 at Saturday Compline, Tone 3.
[^2326]: Daniel 3:1-23, Song of the Three Holy Children 1-29.
[^2327]: Exodus 3:2 (the burning bush unconsumed). Spans physical leaves p262 and p263.
[^2328]: Conclusion of Ode 7, Troparion 1, celebrating the virginal childbearing (*virginitas in partu*).
[^2329]: Judges 6:36-40 (Gideon's fleece); Psalm 71:6 [LXX 71:6: 'He shall come down like rain upon the fleece'].
[^2330]: Penitential supplication for deliverance from Gehenna through the tears of repentance.
[^2331]: Epigraphic iconographic witness: 'чтꙋ́ще, ꙗ҆́кѡ пе́рвый ѡ҆́бразъ' affirms the Seventh Ecumenical Council (Nicaea II) teaching that honor rendered unto the icon passes over to the prototype.
[^2332]: Daniel 3:19-28; Song of the Three Holy Children 34.
[^2333]: Song of Songs 2:2 ('As a lily among thorns, so is my companion among the daughters').
[^2334]: Spans physical leaves p263 and p264; orthodox Christological definition of the Incarnation without confusion of essences.
[^2335]: Compline Canon, Ode 8, Troparion at Glory.
[^2336]: Liturgical witness to iconodulia: Christ is circumscribable and depictable in holy icons (*въ не́йже ви́димъ є҆́сть писа́ньми*) according to His incarnate human nature.
[^2337]: Ezekiel 44:1-3 (the shut gate of the sanctuary through which the Lord alone entered and departed).
[^2338]: The righteous Ancestors of God Joachim and Anna and the Nativity of the Theotokos.
[^2339]: Troparion 2 of Ode 9, spanning physical leaves p263 and p264.
[^2340]: Continuation of Troparion 2, Ode 9 of Compline Canon; 'by the Father's finger' echoes Luke 11:20 and Exodus 31:18.
[^2341]: Penitential troparion before the dread tribunal and Gehenna.
[^2342]: Concluding Theotokion of the Saturday Compline Canon in Tone 3.
[^2343]: Rubric concluding Saturday Compline; the Resurrectional Hypakoe of Tone 3 ('Astonishing by his appearance...') is chanted after the Trisagion prayers.
[^2344]: The Sunday Midnight Office (*Полꙋ́нощница*) begins here with the Canon to the Holy Trinity.
[^2345]: Acrostic in Slavonic: 'Хвалю̀ Тро́ице тя̀, є҆ди́но бгонача́лїе' (Greek: Αἰνῶ σε Τριὰς ἑνικὴ θεαρχία). Composed by St. Metrophanes of Smyrna (9th c.).
[^2346]: Ode 1 Heirmos from the Heirmologion (Tone 3), echoing Exodus 14:21-22 and 15:1.
[^2347]: Liturgical refrain for the Sunday Midnight Office Trinity Canon.
[^2348]: Troparion 1 of Ode 1: invocation of the tri-radiant light of the Holy Trinity.
[^2349]: Troparion 2 of Ode 1: cosmic symphony of angels and men praising the Holy Trinity.
[^2350]: Trinitarian theology of the Cappadocian Fathers (Mind, Word, and Spirit; Unbegotten Father, Begotten Son, Proceeding Spirit).
[^2351]: Theotokion of Ode 1, spanning physical leaves p264 and p265.
[^2352]: Conclusion of Ode 1 Theotokion of the Trinity Canon at Sunday Midnight Office.
[^2353]: Ode 3 Heirmos of Tone 3, highlighting the Trinitarian creation of all things from nothing (*ex nihilo*).
[^2354]: 3 Kings 18:33-34 [1 Kings 18:34 Masoretic]: Elijah commands water to be poured upon the wood a third time on Mount Carmel.
[^2355]: Supplication of human nature to the changeless Trinity.
[^2356]: The harmonic testimony of Prophets and Apostles to the Trinity.
[^2357]: Theotokion of Ode 3, connecting the Incarnation with the revelation of the Holy Trinity.
[^2358]: Sessional Hymn after Ode 3 at the Sunday Midnight Office; Tone 3 Podoben *Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства* ('Beholding the beauty of thy virginity').
[^2359]: Concluding Sessional Theotokion, reflecting fear of the Day of Judgment.
[^2360]: Ode 4 Heirmos from the Heirmologion (Tone 3), Habakkuk 3:2.
[^2361]: Troparion 1 of Ode 4, using patristic botanical metaphors (root and shoots/branches) for the Trinitarian persons, spanning physical leaves p265 and p266.
[^2362]: Conclusion of Troparion 1 of Ode 4; the rubric 'Два́жды' (*Twice*) indicates repeated chanting in the canon.
[^2363]: The choir of incorporeal minds chanting the Trisagion before the super-essential Trinity (*Тро́йце пресꙋ́щная* / Greek: Τριὰς ὑπερούσιε).
[^2364]: Ode 4 Theotokion, confessing the revelation of the Trinity through Christ's Incarnation.
[^2365]: Ode 5 Heirmos (Tone 3), Isaiah 6:1-5.
[^2366]: The seraphic Trisagion ('Holy, Holy, Holy') of Isaiah 6:3 interpreted as the revelation of the Holy Trinity (tri-solar Monad and tri-radiant Essence).
[^2367]: Ode 5 Troparion at Glory, addressing the tri-solar Monad (*є҆ди́нице трисо́лнечнаѧ*).
[^2368]: Complete Dyothelite and Chalcedonian confession: 'in twofold essences and wills' (*въ сꙋщества́хъ и҆ хотѣ́нїихъ сꙋгꙋ́быхъ*), echoing the Sixth Ecumenical Council (Constantinople III).
[^2369]: Ode 6 Heirmos (Tone 3), Matthew 14:30-31.
[^2370]: Genesis 18:1-15: the Hospitality of Abraham at the Oak of Mamre interpreted as a figure of the Holy Trinity.
[^2371]: Troparion 2 of Ode 6, spanning physical leaves p266 and p267.
[^2372]: Ode 6 Theotokion of the Sunday Midnight Office Trinity Canon.
[^2373]: Sessional Hymn after Ode 6 at the Sunday Midnight Office; Tone 3 Podoben *Дѣ́ва дне́сь* ('Today the Virgin').
[^2374]: Concluding Sessional Theotokion, invoking the repentance of the Publican (Luke 18:13) and the Harlot (Luke 7:37-38).
[^2375]: Ode 7 Heirmos of Tone 3, Daniel 3:49-50, Song of the Three Holy Children 29-34.
[^2376]: Prayer to become a cleansed temple of the tri-radiant Godhead.
[^2377]: Strict theological precision regarding the one divine essence (*зра́къ є҆ди́нъ*) and three distinct hypostatic properties (*раздѣ́льныхъ сво́йствахъ*).
[^2378]: Genesis 18:1-14: the promise and reward of the birth of Isaac given to Abraham and Sarah.
[^2379]: Ode 7 Theotokion: the deification (*theosis* / *ѡ҆боже́нїе*) of human nature accomplished through the Incarnation.
[^2380]: Ode 8 Heirmos from the Heirmologion (Tone 3), Daniel 3:51-90. Spans onto physical leaf p268.
[^2381]: Ode 8, Troparion 1 of the Sunday Midnight Office Trinity Canon.
[^2382]: Troparion at Glory, Ode 8; angelic choirs praising the consubstantial Trinity.
[^2383]: Ode 8 Theotokion, concluding the 8th Ode.
[^2384]: Ode 9 Heirmos (Tone 3), Ezekiel 44:1-3.
[^2385]: Seraphic contemplation of the unapproachable divine light; rubric 'Два́жды' (*Twice*).
[^2386]: The human vocation of rational praise from the heart (*ѹ҆́мнꙋю дꙋ́шꙋ и҆ слове́снꙋю*).
[^2387]: Concluding Theotokion of the Sunday Midnight Office Trinity Canon.
[^2388]: Concluding rubrics of the Sunday Midnight Office: the Trinitarian hymns of St. Gregory of Sinai (c. 1265–1346), followed by the Megalynarion, Trisagion, Penitential Troparia, Litany, and Dismissal.
[^2389]: The Sunday Matins (*Оу҆́треня*) office begins here.
[^2390]: Psalm 117:27 [LXX 117:26-27]: 'God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us; blessed is he that comes in the name of the Lord.'
[^2391]: Incipits of the Tone 3 Resurrectional Troparion (*Да веселя́тся небе́сная*) and its Theotokion (*Тя̀ хода́тайствовавшꙋю спасенїе*), chanted earlier at Saturday Vespers (see fols. 253-254 / physical leaves p259-p260).
[^2392]: The 2nd and 3rd Kathismata of the Psalter appointed for Sunday Matins in the weekly cycle.
[^2393]: 1 Corinthians 15:20 ('the firstfruits of them that slept') and Colossians 1:15 ('the firstborn of all creation').
[^2394]: Psalm 9:33 [LXX 9:33; Masoretic Ps. 10:12].
[^2395]: Patristic imagery of Hades bewailing its defeat before the hidden divinity of Christ in the tomb.
[^2396]: Luke 1:28 (the Annunciation of Gabriel to the Virgin).
[^2397]: Sessional Hymn after the 2nd Kathisma; Tone 3 Podoben *Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства* ('Beholding the beauty of thy virginity'). Hebrews 2:9 ('that He by the grace of God should taste death for every man').
[^2398]: Psalm 9:2 [LXX 9:2; Masoretic Ps. 9:1].
[^2399]: 1 Corinthians 15:53 ('this corruptible must put on incorruption').
[^2400]: Concluding Theotokion of the 2nd Kathisma Sessional hymns; Luke 1:28 (*Χαῖρε, κεχαριτωμένη* / *Ра́дꙋйся Благода́тная*).
[^2401]: The 17th Kathisma (Psalm 118, the *Amomos* / *Непоро́чны*) or the Polyeleos on feast days.
[^2402]: Luke 24:5 ('Why seek ye the living among the dead?'); Psalm 65:3 [LXX 65:3; Masoretic Ps. 66:3: 'Say unto God, How awesome are Thy works!'].
[^2403]: The Gradual Antiphons (*Степе́нна* / Greek: Ἀναβαθμοί), based on the Psalms of Ascents (Psalms 119-133 LXX).
[^2404]: Psalm 125:1 [LXX 125:1]: 'When the Lord turned again the captivity of Zion...'
[^2405]: Psalm 125:4-6 [LXX 125:4-6]: 'Turn again our captivity, O Lord, as the streams in the south. They that sow in tears shall reap in joy.'
[^2406]: The Trinitarian doxasticon concluding Antiphon 1; Acts 17:28 ('in Him we live, and move, and have our being').
[^2407]: Psalm 126:1 [LXX 126:1]: 'Except the Lord build the house, they labor in vain that build it: except the Lord keep the city, the watchman wakes but in vain.'
[^2408]: Romans 8:14-17 (the Spirit of adoption); Psalm 126:3 [LXX 126:3: 'the fruit of the womb is his reward'].
[^2409]: Trinitarian doxasticon of Antiphon 2, confessing the creative and vivifying divinity of the Holy Spirit.
[^2410]: Psalm 127:1-2 [LXX 127:1-2]: 'Blessed is every one that fears the Lord; that walks in His ways. For thou shalt eat the labor of thine hands.'
[^2411]: Psalm 127:3 [LXX 127:3]: 'thy children like olive plants round about thy table'; 1 Peter 5:4 ('when the Chief Shepherd shall appear').
[^2412]: Trinitarian doxasticon of Antiphon 3, confessing the Holy Spirit co-worshipped with Father and Word.
[^2413]: Psalm 95:10 [LXX 95:10]: 'Say among the heathen that the Lord reigns: the world also shall be established that it shall not be moved.'
[^2414]: Psalm 95:1 [LXX 95:1]: 'O sing unto the Lord a new song: sing unto the Lord, all the earth.'

[^2424]: Orthodox Christological confession: the divine and human natures united without confusion in the single hypostasis of the incarnate Word born of the Virgin.

[^2523]: The concluding litanies (Litany of Fervent Supplication, Litany of Completion) and the Sunday Matins solemn Dismissal.

[^2613]: Theotokion after the 3rd Stichologia; Luke 1:28 ('Hail, thou that art highly favored [Rejoice, full of grace], the Lord is with thee').

[^2615]: Heirmos of Ode 1, Canon 1; Exodus 14:26-31, 15:1-19.

[^2618]: Canon 1, Ode 1, 2nd Troparion; Jeremiah 7:11; Matthew 21:13 ('den of thieves / robbers'); Luke 2:7 (the cave-manger).
[^2619]: Canon 1, Ode 1, 1st Martyrikon; Hebrews 6:19 ('an anchor of the soul, both sure and steadfast').

[^2717]: Beatitudes Troparion 2.

[^2729]: Sticheron 2 to the Forerunner. Scriptural references to Isaiah 40:3, Malachi 3:1, and Luke 1:76, 7:27.
[^2730]: Sticheron 3 to the Forerunner, celebrating St. John the Baptist as the angel of the desert, completion of the prophets, proto-martyr of the New Covenant, and preacher to those in Hades prior to Christ's descent.
[^2731]: Theotokion on "Lord, I have cried."

[^2887]: Canon 1, Ode 9, Martyrikon 1.
[^2888]: Canon 1, Ode 9, Martyrikon 2: The spiritual Sion (Hebrews 12:22).
[^2889]: Canon 1, Ode 9, Theotokion: The prophetic vision of the light-bearing cloud (Isaiah 19:1); Christ the Sun of Righteousness (Malachi 4:2 [LXX 3:20]).
[^2890]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the second Canon (to the Holy Forerunner): Exodus 13:2, 12; Luke 2:23.
[^2891]: Canon 2, Ode 9, Troparion 1: Peering into the shadow of the Law (Hebrews 10:1).
[^2892]: Canon 2, Ode 9, Troparion 2: The Forerunner as the mediator between the Old and the New Covenants (Matthew 11:13; Luke 16:16).
[^2893]: Canon 2, Ode 9, Troparion 3: The Forerunner as the Friend of the Bridegroom (John 3:29).
[^2894]: Conclusion of Canon 2, Ode 9, Theotokion; physical leaf p312 corresponds to printed folio 310 (т҃і).
[^2895]: Rubric for the conclusion of the Matins canons: It is truly meet (Axion Estin), Litany, Exapostilarion, and Lauds Psalms (Psalms 148–150).
[^2896]: Aposticha Sticheron of Compunction: Peter's tears of repentance (Matthew 26:75), the Publican's sigh (Luke 18:13), and the Harlot's tears (Luke 7:38).
[^2897]: Psalm 89:14, 16 [LXX 89:14, 16].
[^2898]: Aposticha Sticheron of Compunction: Contrition over disparity between outward singing and inward distraction.
[^2899]: Psalm 89:17 [LXX 89:17].
[^2900]: Aposticha Martyrikon: Courageous witness of the martyrs before earthly kings and torturers.
[^2901]: Aposticha Theotokion: Conception without seed by the Holy Spirit (Luke 1:35).
[^2902]: Rubric for the conclusion of Matins: Psalm 91:2 [LXX 91:2], Trisagion prayers, Troparion, Litany, First Hour, and complete daily dismissal.
[^2903]: Beatitudes (Makarismoi) at Liturgy, Troparion 1: The expulsion of Adam from Paradise and the admission of the Penitent Thief (Luke 23:42–43).
[^2904]: Beatitudes Troparion 2: Sincere repentance before Christ Who alone knows the secrets of hearts (Psalm 43:22 [LXX 43:21]).
[^2905]: Conclusion of Beatitudes Troparion 3 (to the Forerunner); physical leaf p313 corresponds to printed folio 311 (т҃аі).
[^2906]: Beatitudes Martyrikon: Fighting the good fight (2 Timothy 4:7).
[^2907]: Beatitudes Triadikon: Dogma of the Holy Trinity, consubstantial and undivided.
[^2908]: Beatitudes Theotokion.
[^2909]: Liturgical rubric / day opening: Tuesday evening initiates the liturgical day of Wednesday, dedicated to the Precious and Life-Giving Cross and the Co-suffering of the Mother of God (Stavrotheotokion). Physical Leaf p313 corresponds to Printed Folio 311 (т҃аі).
[^2910]: Tone 3 Podoben model *Great is the power of Thy martyrs* (*Ве́лїя мꙋ́ченикъ твои́хъ* / *Μεγάλη τῶν Μαρτύρων σου*).
[^2911]: Sticheron 1 of the Cross: Blood from Christ's pierced side (John 19:34) sanctifying creation and delivering Adam.
[^2912]: Sticheron 2 of the Cross: Voluntary poverty of Christ (2 Corinthians 8:9); Christ becoming a curse for us upon the Tree according to the Law (Galatians 3:13; Deuteronomy 21:23).
[^2913]: Sticheron 3 of the Cross: The two fountains of water and blood poured out from the pierced ribs of the Savior (John 19:34).
[^2914]: Conclusion of Stavrotheotokion 1; physical leaf p314 corresponds to printed folio 312 (т҃ві).
[^2915]: Sticheron 2 to the Theotokos: Healing from the spiritual wounds of the primordial serpent (Genesis 3; Luke 10:30).
[^2916]: Sticheron 3 to the Theotokos: The light yoke of Christ (Matthew 11:29–30).
[^2917]: Stavrotheotokion: Simeon's prophecy of the sword piercing the heart of the Mother of God (Luke 2:35).
[^2918]: Aposticha Sticheron 1 of the Cross: The Cross as cosmic guardian, salvation of sinners, mercy seat, and trophy of victory.
[^2919]: Psalm 122:1–2 [LXX 122:1–2].
[^2920]: Aposticha Sticheron 2 of the Cross: Moses' outstretched arms defeating Amalek (Exodus 17:11–12); the sign given to them that fear Him to flee from the face of the bow (Psalm 59:6 [LXX 59:4]).
[^2921]: Psalm 122:3–4 [LXX 122:3–4].
[^2922]: Aposticha Martyrikon: Universal proclamation of the Holy Trinity by Prophets, Apostles, and Martyrs.
[^2923]: Aposticha Stavrotheotokion: The cosmic darkness at the Crucifixion (Matthew 27:45); Christ the never-setting Sun.
[^2924]: Rubric for the conclusion of Tuesday Vespers: Nunc Dimittis, Trisagion prayers, Troparia, Litany, and dismissal.
